# Glossary -- every name in VNB, A to Z

Auto-generated by `(write-glossary-md)`.  1779 names: 30 structure, 9 refinement, 18 instance, 21 view, 695 predicate, 723 functoid, 50 accessor, 25 defined-fn, 57 operator, 10 primitive, 94 tactic, in one alphabetical list.

Every other reference document groups its contents by something --
`OPERATORS.md` by class, `STRUCTURE-INDEX.md` by structure, `TACTICS.md`
by task.  This one has the ordering nobody has to learn.

The RESULTS -- theorems, axioms, supports -- are catalogued separately
(`THEOREMS.md`, `BY-OPERATOR.md`, `PROOF-DEBT.md`); each entry below
instead reports how many results mention the name, which is the
glossary reader's question.  At the REPL: `(glossary 'NAME)`, or
`(glossary "pat")` for a substring.

| kind | what it is |
|---|---|
| `structure` | a declared shape: RING, METRIC-SPACE |
| `refinement` | declared `same-shape-as` another structure |
| `instance` | a named structure value: ZZ-RING, RR-MS |
| `view` | a declared `view-as` bridge |
| `predicate` | IS-RING, CONVERGES, IS-HOM-GROUP |
| `functoid` | a `def-functoid` term constructor |
| `accessor` | a structure slot: CARR, MUL, DIST |
| `defined-fn` | a defined constant or recursion |
| `operator` | a kernel term-former: UNION, NTH, SEP |
| `primitive` | a kernel relation: =, IN, <= |
| `tactic` | an interactive proof command |

**[Symbols](#symbols) [A](#a) [B](#b) [C](#c) [D](#d) [E](#e) [F](#f) [G](#g) [H](#h) [I](#i) [J](#j) [K](#k) [L](#l) [M](#m) [N](#n) [O](#o) [P](#p) [Q](#q) [R](#r) [S](#s) [T](#t) [U](#u) [V](#v) [W](#w) [X](#x) [Y](#y) [Z](#z) **

### `-`  *(operator)*

a term-forming head

characterized by `is-pole`  *(definitional)*:

```
forall([mrf_, mra_, mrn_], is-pole(mrf_, mra_, mrn_) iff mra_ in cc and mrn_ in nn and 1 <= mrn_ and forsome([mrr_ in rr], 0 < mrr_ and forsome([mrg_], holomorphic-on(ball(cc-ms, mra_, mrr_), mrg_) and not(mrg_(mra_) = 0) and forall([mrz_ in difference(ball(cc-ms, mra_, mrr_), singleton(mra_))], mrf_(mrz_) = recip(mrz_ - mra_) ^ mrn_ * mrg_(mrz_)))))
```


### `abelian-group-as-monoid(s)`  *(view)*

an abelian-group viewed as a monoid

characterized by `mpow-add-abelian-group-as-monoid`  *(definitional)*:

```
forall([r], is-abelian-group(r) implies forall([x in carr(r), j in nn, k in nn], mpow(abelian-group-as-monoid(r), x, j + k) = (opr(r))(mpow(abelian-group-as-monoid(r), x, j), mpow(abelian-group-as-monoid(r), x, k))))
```

### `adj(r, n, a)`  *(functoid)*

reads: adj($3)

definition:

```
matof(succ(n), succ(n), vnb-lambda([adu_, adv_], cartesian(interval(1, succ(n)), interval(1, succ(n))), (mul(r))(mpow(ring-multiplicative-monoid(r), (neg(r))(one(r)), adu_ + adv_), det(r, n, minor(a, adv_, adu_, n)))))
```

characterized by `adj-mul`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([r, n, a], is-commutative-ring(r) implies n in nn implies a in mat(succ(n), succ(n), carr(r)) implies matmul(r, adj(r, n, a), a) = matscale(r, det(r, succ(n), a), identmat(r, succ(n))))
```

also: `mul-adj` `adj-type` `adj-entry` 

[mentioned by 8 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#adj)

### `ae-rel(omega, ca, mu, sf)`  *(functoid)*

reads: equality almost everywhere on $4 with respect to $3

definition:

```
{aep_ in cartesian(sf, sf): forsome([aen_ in set], is-null-set(omega, ca, mu, aen_) and forall([aex_ in omega], not(aex_ in aen_) implies (nth(1, aep_))(aex_) = (nth(2, aep_))(aex_)))}
```

characterized by `ae-rel-in`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([omega, ca, mu, sf, af_ in sf, ag_ in sf, ns_], is-null-set(omega, ca, mu, ns_) implies forall([ax_ in omega], not(ax_ in ns_) implies af_(ax_) = ag_(ax_)) implies [af_, ag_] in ae-rel(omega, ca, mu, sf))
```

also: `ae-rel-out` `ae-rel-unfold` `ae-rel-is-equivalence` `l2-null-rel-is-ae-rel` 

[mentioned by 7 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#ae-rel)

### `ae-setoid(omega, ca, mu, sf)`  *(functoid)*

reads: $4 modulo equality almost everywhere with respect to $3

definition:

```
[sf, ae-rel(omega, ca, mu, sf)]
```

characterized by `ae-setoid-is-setoid`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([omega, ca, mu, sf], is-measure(omega, ca, mu) implies sf in set implies sf subset fun(omega, rr) implies is-setoid(ae-setoid(omega, ca, mu, sf)))
```

also: `l2-setoid-is-ae-setoid` 

[mentioned by 5 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#ae-setoid)

### `aff-der(hmp_, hmq_, hmc_, hmd_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the derivative of the affine segment from $1 to $2 on [$3, $4]

definition:

```
vnb-lambda(hmz_, ccint(hmc_, hmd_), recip(hmd_ - hmc_) * (hmq_ - hmp_))
```

[mentioned by 5 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#aff-der)

### `aff-seg(hmp_, hmq_, hmc_, hmd_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the affine segment from $1 to $2 on [$3, $4]

definition:

```
vnb-lambda(hmz_, ccint(hmc_, hmd_), hmp_ + ((hmz_ - hmc_) * recip(hmd_ - hmc_)) * (hmq_ - hmp_))
```

characterized by `aff-seg-ends`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([p, q, c, d], p in cc implies q in cc implies c in rr implies d in rr implies c < d implies (aff-seg(p, q, c, d))(c) = p and (aff-seg(p, q, c, d))(d) = q)
```

also: `aff-seg-is-road` `aff-seg-in-convex` 

[mentioned by 8 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#aff-seg)

### `ag-zz-module(qag_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the abelian group $1 as a Z-module

definition:

```
[zz-ring, carr(qag_), opr(qag_), iden(qag_), inv(qag_), vnb-lambda([azk_, aza_], cartesian(zz, carr(qag_)), zz-act(qag_, azk_, aza_))]
```

characterized by `ag-zz-module-is-module`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([g], is-abelian-group(g) implies is-module(ag-zz-module(g)))
```

[mentioned by 48 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#ag-zz-module)

### `agree-length(alx_, aly_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the length of the common prefix of $1 and $2

definition:

```
iota(alk_, alk_ in nn and not(alx_(alk_) = aly_(alk_)) and forall([alj_ in nn], alj_ < alk_ implies alx_(alj_) = aly_(alj_)))
```

characterized by `agree-length-sym`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([sx_, xs, ys], xs in fun(nn, sx_) implies ys in fun(nn, sx_) implies not(xs = ys) implies agree-length(xs, ys) = agree-length(ys, xs))
```

also: `agree-length-prop` 

[mentioned by 4 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#agree-length)

### `angle(ang1_, ang2_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the angle from $2 to $1

definition:

```
pi + imag-part(choice({angz_ in cc: -pi <= imag-part(angz_) and imag-part(angz_) < pi and cc-exp(angz_) = -(ang1_ * conjugate(ang2_))}))
```

characterized by `angle-lt-pi-iff`  *(proven)*:

```
forall([anz_, anv_], anz_ in cc implies anv_ in cc implies not(anz_ = 0) implies not(anv_ = 0) implies angle(anz_, anv_) < pi iff 0 < imag-part(anz_ * conjugate(anv_)) or imag-part(anz_ * conjugate(anv_)) = 0 and 0 < real-part(anz_ * conjugate(anv_)))
```

also: `interior-angle-sign` 

[mentioned by 8 result(s)](BY-OPERATOR.md#angle)

### `annulus(anla_, anlr_, anls_)`  *(functoid)*

reads: the open annulus about $1 between the radii $2 and $3

### `calc`  *(tactic)*

`(calc L0 (rel1 L1 [just1]) (rel2 L2 [just2]) ...)` -- Ground the focus goal (REL L0 Ln) by a CHAIN of intermediaries L0 rel1 L1 rel2 L2 ... reln Ln: proves each link and composes them into the endpoint.  A link no lane can close is LEFT OPEN as a leaf -- the refinement point, and the PSS candidate.  Relations: = == (folded by transitivity), < <= (folded through the co-*-trans lemmas, with = steps riding along), IFF (chained per direction).  RETURNS the links, the open ones, and their PSS candidates as an alist.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

```
forall([n, al, be, h], n in nn implies al in fun(interval(1, n), zz) implies be in fun(interval(1, n), zz) implies forall([ifi_ in interval(1, n)], 0 <= al(ifi_)) implies forall([ifi_ in interval(1, n)], 0 <= be(ifi_)) implies forall([ifi_, ifj_], ifi_ in interval(1, n) implies ifj_ in interval(1, n) implies ifi_ <= ifj_ implies zz-divides(al(ifi_), al(ifj_))) implies forall([ifi_, ifj_], ifi_ in interval(1, n) implies ifj_ in interval(1, n) implies ifi_ <= ifj_ implies zz-divides(be(ifi_), be(ifj_))) implies is-iso-module(cyclic-sum(n, al), cyclic-sum(n, be), h) implies al = be)
```

### `dial`  *(tactic)*

`(dial notch)` -- The PRESENTATION RESOLUTION DIAL: how much of the sequent to show.  r0 kernel S-expression, r1 surface syntax (the default, and the last INVERTIBLE notch -- what is printed re-parses to what was printed), r2 guards and typing hypotheses elided, r3 also structures destructured to their declared slot names and deep subterms elided.  (dial) reports the notch, (dial n) sets it, (dial 'auto) reads the current goal and suggests one, (dial 'why) shows the score behind the suggestion.  For ONE FORMULA rather than the proof state, (dial-wff x [n]) -- x a wff, a "surface string" or an S-expression, n defaulting to the dial's current notch: (dial-wff (lookup-theorem 'matmul-assoc) 3).

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `dial-wff`  *(tactic)*

`(dial-wff x notch)` -- Present ONE FORMULA at a resolution notch, instead of the current proof state: x is a wff, a "surface string" or an S-expression, and notch defaults to the dial's current setting.  (dial-wff (lookup-theorem 'matmul-assoc) 3).  Same rungs, same aperture line, no proof required.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `ew`  *(tactic)*

`(ew term)` -- Existential witness: discharge a FORSOME goal by supplying the witness term.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `ew-poly`  *(tactic)*

`(ew-poly)` -- Close an existential goal forsome([a in C], L = R) (or unguarded) in which a occurs once, linearly: compute the witness by EXACT POLYNOMIAL DIVISION, supply it (ew), type it (type-term) and close the identity (crs).  One command for `(x+y)^3 = x^3 + y*a'.  Declines, with the reason and nothing changed, when the division leaves a remainder.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `exp`  *(operator)*

a term-forming head

### `expand`  *(tactic)*

`(expand)` -- SHOW the expansion as a step: rewrite each polynomial side of an equation in the goal (also inside an existential or a conjunction) to its sum-of-monomials normal form, highest degree first.  (x + y)^3 = x^3 + y*a becomes x^3 + 3*x^2*y + 3*x*y^2 + y^3 = x^3 + y*a.  Declines, with the reason and nothing changed, when no side changes.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `focus`  *(tactic)*

`(focus n)` -- Switch the focus to the n-th open goal (1-based, the POSITION in the list (show) prints: the focus first, then the others).  A position shifts as leaves close; the node number in brackets does not -- see focus-id.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `focus-id`  *(tactic)*

`(focus-id k)` -- Switch the focus to the ungrounded node whose NODE NUMBER is k, the number (show) and the Focus Workspace print in brackets before each sequent.  Any ungrounded node, not only a leaf: a driver records a leaf by position as (focus n) and an ungrounded node a rule has already fired on as (focus-id k), so a page may name either.  Recorded as a step, undone by backup-one.  The move to make when reading a goal off the panel, e.g. the side leaf of a cut: (cut L) opens  G |- L  (the side leaf) and  G, L |- goal  (the main branch), and a composite such as zero-it leaves the focus on whichever it did not close.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`


### `ineq`  *(tactic)*

`(ineq i1 i2 ...)` -- Close a linear-inequality goal over RR (<= < > >= = between RR terms) as a consequence of the named assumptions (1-based indices), via the Fourier-Motzkin/Farkas oracle. ### `keep`  *(tactic)*

`(keep hyp ...)` -- Keep only the cited assumptions and drop every other one, as ONE recorded step.  Each `hyp' may be a formula, a "string", or a 1-based assumption index; they are recorded as formulas.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

## M

### `mac`  *(tactic)*

`(mac 'name)` -- Rewrite the GOAL with an equivalence macete (unfold a definition, apply an iff/=/== law).  Fires only where the macete's side-conditions already hold in context.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `mac-h`  *(tactic)*

`(mac-h 'name hyp)` -- Rewrite a cited ASSUMPTION in place with an equivalence macete; any side-condition not already in context is spawned as a new subgoal.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `mac-h*`  *(tactic)*

`(mac-h*)` -- Saturating mac-h: repeatedly unfold every defined predicate in the hypotheses and split the conjunctions they expose, until nothing is left folded.  One step in the trace instead of a dozen.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `macm`  *(tactic)*

`(macm 'name)` -- Like mac, but a CONDITIONAL macete fires even when its side-conditions are not yet in context: each unmet condition is left as a new subgoal.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `preamble`  *(tactic)*

`(preamble)  |  (preamble 'NAME)  |  (preamble "file.pre")` -- Apply an editable RULE FILE to the focus goal, one step at a time: each rule reads `if the sequent looks like this, do that', and the first rule that matches AND makes progress is kept; the loop repeats on the new focus until the goal is done, no rule applies, or 40 steps.  Rules come from ~/.vnb-preamble.pre when it exists, else the shipped preambles/default.pre (peel, split, close by context, reflexivity, arithmetic, type-term, zero-it, ineq, beta, unfold, and a tentative cut).  Prints one report: what each rule did, which rules matched but were rejected, and where it stopped and why.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `prop`  *(tactic)*

`(prop)` -- Decide the goal by PROPOSITIONAL logic from the context, and close it if it follows.  Every non-connective formula -- `x in a', an equation, a whole `forall(...)' -- is one opaque atom; AND/OR/NOT/IMPLIES/IFF are read.  On a goal that does NOT follow it prints the countermodel (which atom must be true, which false) and leaves the proof untouched.  Adds no trust: it decides semantically, then discharges through di/ai/oi/ass/use-em/have!/detach!, so the bill is unchanged and the recorded script is the ordinary step-by-step proof.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `scout`  *(tactic)*

`(scout [depth [branch [nodes]]])` -- Speculatively search to a bounded depth across INDEPENDENT scratch branches; RETURNS a nested list (number-of-branches (d b) goal best-partials closing-branches) rather than printing.  Never touches the live proof.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `scout-run`  *(tactic)*

`(scout-run k)` -- Adopt closing branch k from the last (scout)/(scout-show) onto the live proof, running its tactics for real (they record and display normally).

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `scout-show`  *(tactic)*

`(scout-show [depth [branch [nodes]]])` -- Like (scout), but PRINTS the human-readable report (examined count, goal, closing branches or best partials) to the REPL.  Returns the same nested list (scout) does.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `show-graph`  *(tactic)*

`(show-graph)` -- Print EVERY node of the current proof, closed ones included: one line per sequent node in creation order (the bracketed number the panel prints, the sequent, [GROUNDED] when justified), and under each node one line per inference into it -- the rule and the numbers of the hypothesis nodes it rests on.  The focus and the open leaves are marked.  This is where a branch a composite closed is found: after (cut L), the side leaf G |- L and the main branch G, L |- goal are both nodes, and zero-it on an existential closes the main one.  Records nothing; the [Graph] button of the Focus Workspace shows it.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `simp`  *(tactic)*

`(simp [target])` -- Rewrite a commutative-ring SUBTERM of the goal to canonical form, IN PLACE (e.g. (x+y)^2 inside a larger goal becomes x^2 + 2*x*y + y^2).  Works on BOTH surfaces: concrete number domains (+ * - ^ over NN/ZZ/QQ/RR/CC) and a generic ring s ((ADD s)/(MUL s)/(NEG s), carrier (CARR s)).  No arg = outermost ring subterm; "term" targets a specific one.  Sound by cut + crs + eq-subst (no new kernel rule); needs the subterm's generators typed in context (true post-di), else refuses and names them.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `supply`  *(tactic)*

`(supply)` -- Close an inequality goal the way a hand proof would: beta-reduce any applied lambda, land the typing certificates and the standard bounds the linear oracle cannot see -- including the TRIANGLE inequality at the summands of an abs of a sum -- then call ineq over every premise.  Rehearses the whole sequence on a throwaway copy and does NOTHING unless it closes; on a miss it prints the sequence it tried.  Adds no trust: it drives lam-b, fact and ineq, each of which records itself.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `type-term`  *(tactic)*

`(type-term)` -- Close a typing goal (IN t C).  In a number class (NN ZZ QQ RR CC) t may be built from context-typed atoms (or atoms typed in a subclass: NN in ZZ, ZZ in RR, ...), numerals, + * -, and powers, typed by the class's closure laws, bottom-up.  In ANY class C, t may be an atom typed in a subclass, or an application f(a) with f in fun(A, C) in context and a typable in A, recursively: f(3 * x + 1) in B from f in fun(zz, B) and x in zz.  Declines -- with a warning, and nothing changed -- when there is no plan.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`

### `zero-it`  *(tactic)*

`(zero-it)` -- Move an equation goal P = Q (or P == Q, bare or under typed universals) to R = 0, where R is the normal form of P - Q (crs's calculator, highest degree first); prints `P - Q normalises to R' and returns R.  An inequality goal P <= Q or P < Q (the order of RR; atoms typed in NN, ZZ or QQ are lifted) is moved to 0 <= R or 0 < R, R the normal form of Q - P, the branch closed through rr-le-from-diff-nonneg / rr-lt-from-diff-pos; a numeral R decides it (0 <= -1: FALSE, nothing changed; 0 < 2: closed).  CC and an abstract ring have no order and are declined.  R = 0: closed by crs.  R a non-zero numeral: says the goal is FALSE and changes nothing.  Otherwise ONE recorded step leaves the goal R = 0, the atoms' typings landed or listed as owed side leaves.

declared in `tactics-help.scm`
